严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在 PBMC 中诱导强大的 抗病毒药物 先天免疫,灭活病毒部分反映这种免疫

SARS-CoV-2 induces robust antiviral innate immunity in PBMCs that is partially mirrored by inactivated virus
作者： Ronald J. Jacobi, Jelena Cvetkovic, Caroline R.M. Wiggers, Hasan Balci, Dries Heylen, Alberto Miranda-Bedate, Elena Pinelli, Martina Kutmon, Martijn D. B. van de Garde, Jelena Cvetković
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The COVID-19 pandemic highlighted the urgent need to understand SARS-CoV-2 immunogenicity and pathogenesis to guide diagnostics, therapeutics, and vaccines. However, studying live virus requires high-containment facilities, prompting interest in inactivated virus as a safer model.
设计与对象
这项体外研究比较了暴露于热灭活 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 和活 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的外周血单核细胞 (PBMC) 的 免疫应答。该研究使用单细胞分析和转录组分析来评估先天免疫激活。
核心方法
将 PBMC 暴露于热灭活或活 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,然后进行单细胞分析以量化单核细胞活化和细胞因子分泌。暴露后数小时进行转录组分析,以评估 抗病毒药物 和炎症通路中的基因富集。
主要结果
单核细胞是暴露于灭活病毒的 PBMC 中炎症和 抗病毒药物 先天 抗病毒药物 的主要贡献者。与暴露于灭活病毒的PBMC相比,暴露于活病毒的PBMC表现出更强烈的活化单核细胞表型,并且分泌显着更高的细胞因子水平。灭活病毒和活病毒均诱导与 抗病毒药物 和炎症途径相关的基因相似的富集。
解释与创新
该研究表明,热灭活的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 在免疫细胞中触发基本的 抗病毒药物 转录程序,与活病毒相当,尽管活病毒引发更强大的细胞激活和细胞因子反应。这种区别强调了灭活病毒模型和活病毒模型之间免疫激活的功能差异。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,灭活病毒可能是研究核心 抗病毒药物 反应的有用替代品,但可能不足以代表活病毒诱导的全谱免疫激活。这对免疫学测定和 疫苗 开发具有影响,其中必须考虑功能差异。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到体外设计的限制,可能无法完全捕捉体内 免疫应答 的复杂性。此外,使用热灭活病毒可能无法完全复制活病毒的免疫原性,可能限制研究结果的普遍性。由于这些限制,证据是中等的。

SARS-CoV-2 induces robust antiviral innate immunity in PBMCs that is partially mirrored by inactivated virus

Authors: Ronald J. Jacobi, Jelena Cvetkovic, Caroline R.M. Wiggers, Hasan Balci, Dries Heylen, Alberto Miranda-Bedate, Elena Pinelli, Martina Kutmon, Martijn D. B. van de Garde, Jelena Cvetković
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic, caused by SARS-CoV-2, has underscored the importance of understanding viral immunogenicity and pathogenesis in order to develop effective diagnostics, therapeutics, and vaccines. Although studying novel, highly pathogenic viruses requires high-containment facilities, inactivated SARS-CoV-2 provides a non-infectious model to safely investigate various aspects of the immune response induced by the virus. While inactivated SARS-CoV-2 has been shown to evoke immune responses, it remains unclear to what extent these responses mimic those induced by live virus. In this study, we investigated the in vitro immune response of peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) to heat‐inactivated and live SARS-CoV-2. Single‐cell analysis of PBMCs exposed to inactivated virus showed that monocytes were the predominant contributors to the inflammatory and antiviral innate immune responses. PBMCs exposed to live virus exhibited a more strongly activated monocyte phenotype and secreted significantly higher levels of cytokines than those exposed to inactivated virus. Nevertheless, transcriptomic analysis several hours post‐exposure revealed that both inactivated and live virus induced a similar enrichment of genes associated with antiviral and inflammatory pathways. These findings highlight that while heat-inactivated SARS-CoV-2 triggers fundamental antiviral transcriptional programs in immune cells similar to live virus, live virus induces a more robust cellular activation and cytokine response. This distinction has important implications for the use of inactivated virus in immunological assays and vaccine development, emphasizing the need to take these functional differences in immune activation into account when interpreting results and designing interventions.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The COVID-19 pandemic highlighted the urgent need to understand SARS-CoV-2 immunogenicity and pathogenesis to guide diagnostics, therapeutics, and vaccines. However, studying live virus requires high-containment facilities, prompting interest in inactivated virus as a safer model.
Design and population
This in vitro study compared the immune responses of peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs) exposed to heat-inactivated SARS-CoV-2 versus live SARS-CoV-2. The study used single-cell analysis and transcriptomic profiling to assess innate immune activation.
Core methods
PBMCs were exposed to either heat-inactivated or live SARS-CoV-2, followed by single-cell analysis to quantify monocyte activation and cytokine secretion. Transcriptomic profiling was performed several hours post-exposure to evaluate gene enrichment in antiviral and inflammatory pathways.
Main results
Monocytes were the predominant contributors to inflammatory and antiviral innate immune responses in PBMCs exposed to inactivated virus. PBMCs exposed to live virus showed a more strongly activated monocyte phenotype and secreted significantly higher cytokine levels than those exposed to inactivated virus. Both inactivated and live virus induced similar enrichment of genes associated with antiviral and inflammatory pathways.
Interpretation and novelty
The study demonstrates that heat-inactivated SARS-CoV-2 triggers fundamental antiviral transcriptional programs in immune cells comparable to live virus, though live virus elicits a more robust cellular activation and cytokine response. This distinction underscores the functional differences in immune activation between inactivated and live virus models.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that inactivated virus may be a useful surrogate for studying core antiviral responses but may underrepresent the full spectrum of immune activation induced by live virus. This has implications for immunological assays and vaccine development, where functional differences must be considered.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its in vitro design, which may not fully capture the complexity of in vivo immune responses. Additionally, the use of heat-inactivated virus may not fully replicate the immunogenicity of live virus, potentially limiting the generalisability of the findings. The evidence is moderate due to these constraints.

新加坡儿童和年轻人感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 后新发心理健康诊断的风险:一项回顾性 队列研究 研究。

Risk of new-incident mental health diagnoses following SARS-CoV-2 infection amongst children and young adults in Singapore: a retrospective cohort study.
作者： Liang En Wee, Jue Tao Lim, Janice Yu Jin Tan, Alex Jie Wei Yap, Calvin J. Chiew, Say How Ong, Choon Guan Lim, Daniel Shuen Sheng Fung, David Chien Lye, Kelvin Bryan Tan
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to investigate the risk of new-incident mental health diagnoses following SARS-CoV-2 infection in children and young adults, as mental health disorders increased post-pandemic but direct attribution to acute infection remained challenging due to the absence of linkage to COVID-19 registries.
设计与对象
一项回顾性 队列研究 研究是利用新加坡的医疗保健索赔数据库进行的,评估了 2012 年 1 月 1 日至 2024 年 2 月 29 日期间 6-25 岁儿童和年轻人新发生的精神健康障碍。
核心方法
Negative binomial regression was used to model the influence of SARS-CoV-2 infection on monthly observed new-incident mental health disorders, accounting for seasonality and overall increasing trends. Separate linkage with the national COVID-19 database enabled the construction of contemporaneous SARS-CoV-2-infected and test-negative cohorts. Cox regression with overlap weights estimated risks of new-incident medically-diagnosed mental health disorders 31-300 days post-infection versus test-negatives.
主要结果
与测试阴性者 (N = 482,588) 相比,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染儿童/青少年 (N = 456,034) 中任何新发生的医学诊断精神健康障碍的风险没有显着增加 (调整后的风险比,aHR = 1.01 [95% CI = 0.90-1.12]),压力/焦虑症除外(aHR = 1.20 [95% CI = 1.02-1.40])。大流行遏制阶段的感染与压力/焦虑风险显着增加有关,但在取消限制的大流行恢复阶段则不然。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,如观察到的关联所示,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后儿童和年轻人在大流行遏制阶段发生压力/焦虑症的风险增加可能是由初始感染严重程度和并发家庭感染介导的。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果深入了解了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染在特定大流行阶段对儿童和年轻人的潜在心理健康影响,这可以为公共卫生危机期间有针对性的监测和心理健康支持干预措施提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对医疗保健索赔数据库的依赖而受到限制,该数据库可能无法涵盖所有​​心理健康诊断。证据是中等的,因为研究结果基于观察数据并且没有建立因果关系。

Risk of new-incident mental health diagnoses following SARS-CoV-2 infection amongst children and young adults in Singapore: a retrospective cohort study.

Authors: Liang En Wee, Jue Tao Lim, Janice Yu Jin Tan, Alex Jie Wei Yap, Calvin J. Chiew, Say How Ong, Choon Guan Lim, Daniel Shuen Sheng Fung, David Chien Lye, Kelvin Bryan Tan
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Mental-health-disorders in children/young adults increased post-pandemic; however, the absence of linkage to COVID-19 registries made direct attribution of psychological sequelae to acute infection challenging. METHODS: Healthcare-claims databases in Singapore were utilised to evaluate rates of new-incident mental-health-disorders in children/young adults aged 6-25 years from January 1st, 2012 to 29th February, 2024. Influence on monthly observed new-incident mental-health-disorders across various pandemic phases was modelled using negative binomial regression, accounting for seasonality and overall increasing trend (year). Separate linkage with the national COVID-19 database allowed construction of contemporaneous SARS-CoV-2-infected/test-negative cohorts. Cox regression, with overlap weights applied, was utilised to estimate risks of new-incident medically-diagnosed mental-health-disorders 31-300 days post-infection versus test-negatives. RESULTS: Overall, there was no significant increase in risk for any new-incident medically-diagnosed mental-health-disorders (adjusted-hazards-ratio, aHR = 1.01 [95% CI = 0.90-1.12]) amongst SARS-CoV-2-infected children/young adults (N = 456,034) versus test-negatives (N = 482,588); with the sole exception that of stress/anxiety disorders (aHR = 1.20 [95% CI = 1.02-1.40]). Only infections during the pandemic-containment-phase were associated with significantly increased risk of stress/anxiety, but not during the pandemic-resiliency-phase when restrictions were lifted (pandemic-containment: aHR = 1.25 [95% CI = 1.02-1.54]; pandemic-resiliency: aHR = 1.15 [95% CI = 0.89-1.48]). Risk of stress/anxiety was elevated amongst COVID-19 cases with a concurrently-infected adult household contact. A 10% increase in new-incident mental-health-disorders was observed amongst Singaporean children/young adults during the pandemic-containment-phase, but this subsequently normalised in the pandemic-resiliency-phase. CONCLUSIONS: Increase in medically-diagnosed mental-health-disorders amongst Singaporean children/young adults at the height of pandemic restrictions did not persist into endemicity. Increased incidence of medically-diagnosed stress/anxiety post-COVID-19 in children/young adults at the pandemic height may be mediated by initial infection severity and concurrent household infection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the risk of new-incident mental health diagnoses following SARS-CoV-2 infection in children and young adults, as mental health disorders increased post-pandemic but direct attribution to acute infection remained challenging due to the absence of linkage to COVID-19 registries.
Design and population
A retrospective cohort study was conducted using healthcare-claims databases in Singapore, evaluating new-incident mental health disorders in children and young adults aged 6-25 years from January 1st, 2012 to 29th February, 2024.
Core methods
Negative binomial regression was used to model the influence of SARS-CoV-2 infection on monthly observed new-incident mental health disorders, accounting for seasonality and overall increasing trends. Separate linkage with the national COVID-19 database enabled the construction of contemporaneous SARS-CoV-2-infected and test-negative cohorts. Cox regression with overlap weights estimated risks of new-incident medically-diagnosed mental health disorders 31-300 days post-infection versus test-negatives.
Main results
There was no significant increase in risk for any new-incident medically-diagnosed mental health disorders (adjusted-hazards-ratio, aHR = 1.01 [95% CI = 0.90-1.12]) among SARS-CoV-2-infected children/young adults (N = 456,034) versus test-negatives (N = 482,588), except for stress/anxiety disorders (aHR = 1.20 [95% CI = 1.02-1.40]). Infections during the pandemic-containment-phase were associated with a significantly increased risk of stress/anxiety, but not during the pandemic-resiliency-phase when restrictions were lifted.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that the increased risk of stress/anxiety disorders among children and young adults post-SARS-CoV-2 infection during the pandemic-containment-phase may be mediated by initial infection severity and concurrent household infection, as indicated by the observed association.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results provide insights into the potential mental health impacts of SARS-CoV-2 infection in children and young adults during specific pandemic phases, which could inform targeted surveillance and interventions for mental health support during public health crises.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on healthcare-claims databases, which may not capture all mental health diagnoses. The evidence is moderate, as the findings are based on observational data and do not establish causality.

Children's social connections, internet usage and mental health during the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South: evidence from Disrupting Harm.

Children's social connections, internet usage and mental health during the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South: evidence from Disrupting Harm.
作者： Metherell TE, Sebastian Kurten, Sakshi Ghai, George B. Ploubidis, Darío Moreno-Agostino, Amy Orben
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to investigate the cross-sectional associations of digital technology use with mental health indicators during the pandemic in 12 countries in the Global South. There is a notable lack of studies investigating the mental health associations of the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South, particularly with regard to the use of digital technologies.
设计与对象
该调查包括埃塞俄比亚、肯尼亚、莫桑比克、纳米比亚、坦桑尼亚、乌干达、柬埔寨、印度尼西亚、马来西亚、菲律宾、泰国和越南的 11,912 名 12 至 17 岁互联网使用儿童的代表性样本。该研究是在大流行期间进行的,重点关注南半球的儿童。
核心方法
我们使用了联合国儿童基金会因诺琴蒂破坏性危害调查的数据。我们使用每个国家(不包括没有实施封锁的坦桑尼亚)的六种不同的福祉指标,对封锁期间的社会联系和大流行期间的互联网使用之间的关联进行了建模,并使用稳健的线性和逻辑回归在总体样本中进行建模。我们控制了一系列假定的社会经济和人口统计学混杂因素,并使用多重插补处理缺失数据。
主要结果
我们没有发现明确的证据表明各国封锁期间社交联系或互联网使用与心理健康指标存在任何一般关联。相反,我们的结果很复杂,并且表明这种关系在很大程度上取决于个人的背景;不仅与他们所居住的国家有关,还与他们的性别、城市化程度和其他因素有关。
解释与创新
我们的结果突出了进一步研究的重点领域。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,数字技术的使用对心理健康的影响因个人和背景因素而异,这可以为有针对性的干预措施和未来的研究方向提供信息。
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据并未报告该研究的具体局限性。由于该研究是横断面的并且没有建立因果关系,因此证据强度中等。

Children's social connections, internet usage and mental health during the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South: evidence from Disrupting Harm.

Authors: Metherell TE, Sebastian Kurten, Sakshi Ghai, George B. Ploubidis, Darío Moreno-Agostino, Amy Orben
Original abstract (English)
Purpose There is a notable lack of studies investigating the mental health associations of the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South, particularly with regard to the use of digital technologies. We aimed to investigate the cross-sectional associations of digital technology use with mental health indicators during the pandemic in 12 countries in the Global South. Methods We used data from the UNICEF Innocenti Disrupting Harm survey. The survey included a representative sample of 11,912 internet-using children aged 12-17 in Ethiopia, Kenya, Mozambique, Namibia, Tanzania, Uganda, Cambodia, Indonesia, Malaysia, Philippines, Thailand and Vietnam. We modelled the associations of social connection during lockdown and internet use during the pandemic with six different indicators of wellbeing in each country (excluding Tanzania, where there had been no lockdowns) and in the overall sample using robust linear and logistic regression. We controlled for a range of putative socioeconomic and demographic confounders and handled missing data using multiple imputation. Results We did not find clear evidence for any general associations of social connection or internet use with mental health indicators during lockdown across countries. Rather, our results are complex and demonstrate that the relationship depends heavily on an individual's context; not just on the country they are living in but also on their gender, urbanicity and other factors. Nonetheless, our results highlight key focus areas for further research. Conclusion Extensive further research is needed to identify ways in which technology use, including internet use, might act as a risk or protective factor relevant to mental ill health among young people in the countries of the Global South.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the cross-sectional associations of digital technology use with mental health indicators during the pandemic in 12 countries in the Global South. There is a notable lack of studies investigating the mental health associations of the COVID-19 pandemic in the Global South, particularly with regard to the use of digital technologies.
Design and population
The survey included a representative sample of 11,912 internet-using children aged 12-17 in Ethiopia, Kenya, Mozambique, Namibia, Tanzania, Uganda, Cambodia, Indonesia, Malaysia, Philippines, Thailand and Vietnam. The study was conducted during the pandemic and focused on children in the Global South.
Core methods
We used data from the UNICEF Innocenti Disrupting Harm survey. We modelled the associations of social connection during lockdown and internet use during the pandemic with six different indicators of wellbeing in each country (excluding Tanzania, where there had been no lockdowns) and in the overall sample using robust linear and logistic regression. We controlled for a range of putative socioeconomic and demographic confounders and handled missing data using multiple imputation.
Main results

Rather, our results are complex and demonstrate that the relationship depends heavily on an individual's context; not just on the country they are living in but also on their gender, urbanicity and other factors.
Interpretation and novelty
Our results highlight key focus areas for further research.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that the impact of digital technology use on mental health varies based on individual and contextual factors, which could inform targeted interventions and future research directions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations of the study. The evidence strength is moderate as the study is cross-sectional and does not establish causality.

急性和 长新冠-19 中的病毒重新激活。

Virus reactivation in acute and long COVID-19.
作者： Cole P. Maguire, Jing Chen, Nadine Rouphael, Brinkley A Morse, Annmarie Hoch, Harry Pickering, Hoang Van Phan, Abigail L. Glascock, Victoria T. Chu, Ravi Dandekar
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究调查了急性期和 长新冠-19 期间慢性病毒的重新激活,解决了在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染背景下病毒重新激活的程度、时间动态和免疫学影响方面的差距。
设计与对象
该研究利用了 1,154 名 新型冠状病毒感染 住院患者的多组学纵向数据,这些患者参加了 新型冠状病毒感染 队列研究 (IMPACC) 研究的免疫表型评估,重点关注急性期和 长新冠-19 期间疱疹病毒科和指环病毒科的重新激活。
核心方法
The researchers analyzed multi-omic data, including immune, transcriptomic and metabolomic profiles, from hospitalized patients with COVID-19 to identify patterns of viral reactivation and its association with disease severity and clinical outcomes.
主要结果
The study found significant reactivation of Herpesviridae and Anelloviridae during acute COVID-19, with distinct temporal dynamics, and demonstrated that reactivation correlates with disease severity, host immune effects and clinical outcomes. It also reported persistence of viral reactivation in convalescence and an association of Anelloviridae with long COVID.
解释与创新
这些发现挑战了慢性病毒再激活主要是免疫抑制的结果的假设,表明它在免疫功能正常的个体中经常发生在严重疾病期间,并且与全身炎症增加有关。
科研与公卫意义
The results could inform future strategies for prognostication and treatment of acute COVID-19 and long COVID by identifying immune, transcriptomic and metabolomic signatures associated with viral reactivation.
局限与证据强度
该研究没有确定病毒重新激活与临床结果之间的因果关系,并且证据基于观察数据,这将结论的强度限制为中等。

Virus reactivation in acute and long COVID-19.

Authors: Cole P. Maguire, Jing Chen, Nadine Rouphael, Brinkley A Morse, Annmarie Hoch, Harry Pickering, Hoang Van Phan, Abigail L. Glascock, Victoria T. Chu, Ravi Dandekar
Original abstract (English)
Chronic viral infections are ubiquitous in humans, with individuals carrying multiple viruses that can reactivate during physiological stress, including severe illness 1 . Notably, SARS-CoV-2 infection has been shown to reactivate chronic viruses such as Epstein-Barr virus and cytomegalovirus, yet the full extent, temporal dynamics and immunological impact of viral reactivation in COVID-19 remain incompletely understood 2-7 . Here, leveraging multi-omic longitudinal data from 1,154 hospitalized patients with COVID-19 from the Immunophenotyping Assessment in a COVID-19 Cohort (IMPACC) study, we reveal significant reactivation of Herpesviridae and Anelloviridae during acute COVID-19, with distinct temporal dynamics for different viruses, and demonstrate that reactivation correlates with disease severity, host immune effects and clinical outcomes. Although our results do not establish causation between virus reactivation and clinical outcomes, we highlight the prevalence of chronic viral reactivation during acute COVID-19 and long COVID. Our findings challenge the prevailing view that chronic viral reactivation is primarily a consequence of immunosuppression, demonstrating that reactivations occur frequently in immunocompetent individuals during severe illness and in association with increased systemic inflammation. Additionally, we demonstrate persistence of viral reactivation in convalescence, and report an association of Anelloviridae with long COVID. This study provides immune, transcriptomic and metabolomic signatures of viral reactivation that could inform future strategies to prognosticate and treat acute COVID-19 and long COVID.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study investigates the reactivation of chronic viruses during acute and long COVID-19, addressing the gap in understanding the extent, temporal dynamics and immunological impact of viral reactivation in the context of SARS-CoV-2 infection.
Design and population
The study utilized multi-omic longitudinal data from 1,154 hospitalized patients with COVID-19 enrolled in the Immunophenotyping Assessment in a COVID-19 Cohort (IMPACC) study, focusing on the reactivation of Herpesviridae and Anelloviridae during acute and long COVID-19.
Core methods
The researchers analyzed multi-omic data, including immune, transcriptomic and metabolomic profiles, from hospitalized patients with COVID-19 to identify patterns of viral reactivation and its association with disease severity and clinical outcomes.
Main results
The study found significant reactivation of Herpesviridae and Anelloviridae during acute COVID-19, with distinct temporal dynamics, and demonstrated that reactivation correlates with disease severity, host immune effects and clinical outcomes. It also reported persistence of viral reactivation in convalescence and an association of Anelloviridae with long COVID.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings challenge the assumption that chronic viral reactivation is mainly a result of immunosuppression, showing that it occurs frequently in immunocompetent individuals during severe illness and is linked to increased systemic inflammation.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results could inform future strategies for prognostication and treatment of acute COVID-19 and long COVID by identifying immune, transcriptomic and metabolomic signatures associated with viral reactivation.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study does not establish causation between virus reactivation and clinical outcomes, and the evidence is based on observational data, which limits the strength of the conclusions to moderate.

针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 nsp3 和 nsp5 的高效 抗病毒药物 候选药物。

Highly potent antiviral drug candidates targeting SARS-CoV-2 nsp3 and nsp5.
作者： Mathilde Galais, Guillaume Herlem, Sandy Haidar Ahmad, Antoine Dutilleul, Sébastien Pasquereau, Ranim El Baba, Maxime Bellefroid, Estelle Plant, Stéphanie Morot-Bizot, Fabien Picaud
摘要中文翻译摘要:面对过去 20 年来出现的与冠状病毒家族病毒相关的三种流行病(2002 年中国的 SARS-CoV、2012 年阿拉伯的 MERS-CoV 和 2019 年全球的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2),新 抗病毒药物 治疗 的识别成为重大公共卫生问题。作为新药开发的一部分,对接、虚拟筛选和分子动力学等多种分子建模工具与数据库相结合,以破译冠状病毒靶点的潜在抑制剂。我们基于 ZINC15 数据库批次的高通量虚拟筛选进行配体对接,然后进行点击化学,对靶向 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 非结构蛋白 3 和 5(nsp3 和 nsp5)的 抗病毒药物 药物进行了基于结构的设计,特别关注模拟潜在体内相互作用的松弛结构。基于上述计算机模拟方法,我们选择了两个对 nsp3 和 nsp5 具有高亲和力结合的小分子,分别命名为 Amb929 和 Amb701。然后,我们评估了 Amb929 和 Amb701 对抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的 抗病毒药物 活性,同时考虑到它们潜在的细胞毒性。尽管两种化合物在体外均抑制 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制,但 Amb929 显示出最有效的抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 活性。在这两种药物中,与 Amb701 相比,Amb929 的细胞毒性较小,在人气道上皮 (HAE) 模型上表现出最佳的抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 反应,在细胞培养中具有高选择性指数,并且与未经治疗的条件相比,对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制具有高度抑制作用,为开发具有非常有效的抗冠状病毒活性的药物铺平了道路。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
在过去20年里,三次与冠状病毒科病毒相关的疫情暴发(2002年中国的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒,2012年阿拉伯半岛的中东呼吸综合征冠状病毒,以及2019年全球的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)凸显出识别新型抗病毒药物 治疗的重要公共卫生意义。

对接、虚拟筛选和分子动力学等多种分子建模工具与数据库相结合,破译了冠状病毒靶标的潜在抑制剂。

主要结果
基于上述计算机模拟方法,我们选择了两个对 nsp3 和 nsp5 具有高亲和力结合的小分子,分别命名为 Amb929 和 Amb701。

尽管两种化合物在体外均抑制 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 复制,但 Amb929 显示出最有效的抗 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 活性。

Highly potent antiviral drug candidates targeting SARS-CoV-2 nsp3 and nsp5.

Authors: Mathilde Galais, Guillaume Herlem, Sandy Haidar Ahmad, Antoine Dutilleul, Sébastien Pasquereau, Ranim El Baba, Maxime Bellefroid, Estelle Plant, Stéphanie Morot-Bizot, Fabien Picaud
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Faced with the emergence of three epidemics linked to a virus from the coronaviridæ family over the last 20 years (SARS-CoV in China in 2002, MERS-CoV in Arabia in 2012, and SARS- CoV-2 worldwide in 2019), the identification of new antiviral treatments is of major public health concern. As part of the development of new drugs, several molecular modeling tools such as docking, virtual screening and molecular dynamics were combined with databases to decipher potential inhibitors of coronavirus targets. We performed a structure-based design of antiviral drugs targeting SARS-CoV-2 non-structural proteins 3 and 5 (nsp3 and nsp5) based on a high-throughput virtual screening of the ZINC15 database tranches for ligand docking followed by click chemistry with a particular attention paid to relaxed structures mimicking potential in vivo interactions. Based on the above in silico approaches, we selected two small molecules with high affinity binding for nsp3 and nsp5 named Amb929 and Amb701, respectively. We then assessed the antiviral activity of both the Amb929 and Amb701 against SARS-CoV-2 infection taking into account their potential cytotoxicity. Although both compounds inhibited SARS-CoV-2 replication in vitro , Amb929 displayed the most efficient anti-SARS-CoV-2 activity. Among these two drugs, Amb929 was less cytotoxic compared to Amb701, demonstrating an optimal anti-SARS-CoV-2 response with a high selectivity index in cell culture and a high inhibition of SARS-CoV-2 replication compared to untreated condition on a model of human airway epithelium (HAE), paving the way for the development of drugs with very potent anti-coronavirus activity.

Faced with the emergence of three epidemics linked to a virus from the coronaviridæ family over the last 20 years (SARS-CoV in China in 2002, MERS-CoV in Arabia in 2012, and SARS-CoV-2 worldwide in 2019), the identification of new antiviral treatments is of major public health concern.

A rare osteonecrosis Kienböck’s disease potentially linked to COVID-19: a case report

A rare osteonecrosis Kienböck’s disease potentially linked to COVID-19: a case report
作者： Hatice Ceylan
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Kienböck's disease (KD) is a rare, progressive avascular necrosis of the lunate bone that can cause chronic wrist pain and functional impairment. The study sought to explore whether COVID-19 infection could be associated with the development of KD, a previously unreported potential complication.
设计与对象
This case report describes a 50-year-old woman hospitalized in August 2020 with PCR-confirmed COVID-19 pneumonia and bilateral ground-glass opacities on chest CT. She had no prior corticosteroid exposure or comorbidities and developed progressive left wrist pain approximately 12 weeks after recovery.
核心方法
The paper reports a single case of KD developing after COVID-19 infection, detailing the patient's clinical course and diagnostic imaging findings. Interventions included autologous cytokine-rich serum injections, pharmacological pain management, and surgical lunate excision with bone grafting due to disease progression.
主要结果
Subsequent imaging demonstrated avascular necrosis of the lunate bone, and this case represents the first reported instance of KD developing after COVID-19 infection.
解释与创新
The authors interpret this case as novel evidence suggesting a potential association between COVID-19 and KD, noting that while avascular necrosis has been reported in other skeletal sites post-COVID-19, lunate involvement had not been previously documented.
科研与公卫意义
The findings imply that clinicians should consider KD in patients presenting with wrist pain following COVID-19, potentially enabling earlier diagnosis and improved functional outcomes.
局限与证据强度
作为单个 病例报告,证据因缺乏对照、样本量小且无法建立因果关系而受到限制;证据的强度较低。

A rare osteonecrosis Kienböck’s disease potentially linked to COVID-19: a case report

Authors: Hatice Ceylan
Original abstract (English)
Kienböck's disease (KD), an avascular necrosis of the lunate bone, is a rare and progressive condition that may lead to chronic wrist pain and functional limitation. While avascular necrosis associated with COVID-19 has been reported in other skeletal sites, involvement of the lunate bone has not previously been reported. This case adds novel evidence that suggests a potential association between COVID-19 and Kienböck's disease. To demonstrate this potential association, this paper reports a case of Kienböck's disease developing following COVID-19 infection in a 50-year-old woman who was hospitalized in August 2020 with PCR-confirmed COVID-19 pneumonia and bilateral ground-glass opacities on chest CT. She had no prior corticosteroid exposure or comorbidities. Approximately 12 weeks after recovery, she developed progressive left wrist pain without any history of trauma. Subsequent imaging demonstrated avascular necrosis of the lunate bone. Interventions included autologous cytokine-rich serum injections, pharmacological pain management, and ultimately surgical lunate excision with bone grafting due to progression of the disease. This case represents the first reported instance of Kienböck's disease developing after COVID-19 infection. Increased clinician awareness may lead to earlier diagnosis and better functional outcomes.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Kienböck's disease (KD) is a rare, progressive avascular necrosis of the lunate bone that can cause chronic wrist pain and functional impairment. The study sought to explore whether COVID-19 infection could be associated with the development of KD, a previously unreported potential complication.
Design and population
This case report describes a 50-year-old woman hospitalized in August 2020 with PCR-confirmed COVID-19 pneumonia and bilateral ground-glass opacities on chest CT. She had no prior corticosteroid exposure or comorbidities and developed progressive left wrist pain approximately 12 weeks after recovery.
Core methods
The paper reports a single case of KD developing after COVID-19 infection, detailing the patient's clinical course and diagnostic imaging findings. Interventions included autologous cytokine-rich serum injections, pharmacological pain management, and surgical lunate excision with bone grafting due to disease progression.
Main results
Subsequent imaging demonstrated avascular necrosis of the lunate bone, and this case represents the first reported instance of KD developing after COVID-19 infection.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret this case as novel evidence suggesting a potential association between COVID-19 and KD, noting that while avascular necrosis has been reported in other skeletal sites post-COVID-19, lunate involvement had not been previously documented.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings imply that clinicians should consider KD in patients presenting with wrist pain following COVID-19, potentially enabling earlier diagnosis and improved functional outcomes.
Limitations and evidence strength
As a single case report, the evidence is limited by lack of controls, small sample size, and inability to establish causality; the strength of evidence is low.

使用基于废水的 流行病学 解释疾病 暴发 监测 的方法学变异:严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的案例研究

Accounting for methodological variabilities in disease outbreak surveillance using wastewater-based epidemiology: a case study with SARS-CoV-2
作者： James Boxall-Clasby, Nicola Ceolotto, Marjorie J. Gibbon, Kishore K. Jagadeesan, Edward J. Feil, Theresa Smith, Megan Robertson, Richard Standerwick, Ruth Barden, Julian Faraway
摘要中文翻译摘要 基于废水的 暴发 已成为社区一级监测健康状况和疾病 暴发 的既定方法。尽管 RNA 定量分析方案已得到广泛实施和优化,但各个阶段(例如存储、样本处理和 PCR 定量)引入的变异性特征仍然有限,包括其对流行病学解释的影响。本研究提出了一个分析框架,用于量化废水中 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RNA 测量整个工作流程的变异性,包括储存稳定性、样品处理和 PCR 定量。该框架是针对两个病毒基因靶点(N1、E-Sarbeco)的大型 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 数据集而开发的,这些数据集与人类相关的 CrAssphage 一起进行了量化,获得了 24 个月内来自四个水回收中心 (WRC) 的总共 20,124 个 RT-qPCR 数据点。该分析表明,样品冷藏比冷冻产生更丰富的流行病学数据,并且 CrAssphage 由于其较高的变异性,作为标准化生物标志物的效用很小。对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 目标的分析变异性进行量化,可以开发综合指标(总估计 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2),从而提高灵敏度并减少未检测,但代价是降低精度。这种新颖的多阶段框架能够提高 WBE 数据的敏感性和解释以及 WBE 方法的表征,在病原体监测方面具有广泛的适用性。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
基于废水的 流行病学 (WBE) 已成为社区一级监测 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的既定方法,但分析阶段的变异性仍然有限,影响了流行病学解释。
设计与对象
这项研究开发了一个分析框架来量化废水中 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RNA 测量的变异性,使用两个病毒基因目标(N1、E-Sarbeco)和人类相关 CrAssphage 的大型数据集,并在 24 个月内从四个水回收中心收集了 20,124 个 RT-qPCR 数据点。
核心方法
该框架描述了存储、样本处理和 PCR 定量过程中引入的变异性,旨在通过综合指标(总估计 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)提高 WBE 数据的灵敏度和解释。
主要结果
样品冷藏比冷冻能产生更丰富的流行病学数据,而 CrAssphage 由于其较高的变异性,被发现作为标准化生物标志物的效用很小。
解释与创新
该研究引入了一种新颖的多阶段框架来表征 WBE 方法的变异性,增强 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 数据的灵敏度和解释,同时减少未检测到。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可以为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 监测 开发更可靠的 WBE 协议提供信息,从而提高社区级疾病监测的准确性。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于仅抽象的分析,虽然它提供了对变异性的见解,但缺乏详细的统计或方法验证限制了证据的强度。

Accounting for methodological variabilities in disease outbreak surveillance using wastewater-based epidemiology: a case study with SARS-CoV-2

Authors: James Boxall-Clasby, Nicola Ceolotto, Marjorie J. Gibbon, Kishore K. Jagadeesan, Edward J. Feil, Theresa Smith, Megan Robertson, Richard Standerwick, Ruth Barden, Julian Faraway
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Wastewater-based surveillance of SARS-CoV-2 has become an established approach for monitoring health status and disease outbreak at the community level. Although analytical protocols for RNA quantification have been widely implemented and optimised, the characterisation of variability introduced across all stages, such as storage, sample processing, and PCR quantification, remained limited, including its impact upon epidemiological interpretation. This study proposes an analytical framework to quantify variability across the complete workflow for SARS-CoV-2 RNA measurement in wastewater, including storage stability, sample processing, and PCR quantification. This framework was developed for a large SARS-CoV-2 dataset of two viral gene targets (N1, E-Sarbeco), which were quantified alongside human-associated CrAssphage for a total of 20,124 RT-qPCR data points derived from four water recycling centres (WRCs) over a 24-month period. This analysis revealed that sample refrigeration yielded richer epidemiological data than freezing, and that CrAssphage had minimal utility as a normalisation biomarker given its higher variability. Quantification of analytical variability for SARS-CoV-2 targets enabled development of a composite metric (Total Estimated SARS-CoV-2), improving sensitivity and reducing non-detects at the expense of reduced precision. This novel multi-stage framework had the capability to improve the sensitivity and interpretation of WBE data and the characterisation of WBE methodologies, with broad applicability for pathogen monitoring.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Wastewater-based epidemiology (WBE) has become an established approach for monitoring SARS-CoV-2 at the community level, but variability across analytical stages remains limited, affecting epidemiological interpretation.
Design and population
This study developed an analytical framework to quantify variability in SARS-CoV-2 RNA measurement in wastewater, using a large dataset of two viral gene targets (N1, E-Sarbeco) alongside human-associated CrAssphage, with 20,124 RT-qPCR data points collected from four water recycling centres over 24 months.
Core methods
The framework characterised variability introduced during storage, sample processing, and PCR quantification, aiming to improve the sensitivity and interpretation of WBE data through a composite metric (Total Estimated SARS-CoV-2).
Main results
Sample refrigeration yielded richer epidemiological data than freezing, and CrAssphage was found to have minimal utility as a normalisation biomarker due to its higher variability.
Interpretation and novelty
The study introduced a novel multi-stage framework to characterise variability in WBE methodologies, enhancing the sensitivity and interpretation of SARS-CoV-2 data while reducing non-detects.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform the development of more reliable WBE protocols for SARS-CoV-2 surveillance, improving the accuracy of community-level disease monitoring.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on an abstract-only analysis, and while it provides insights into variability, the lack of detailed statistical or methodological validation limits the strength of the evidence.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 刺突蛋白 中的保守 QTQTX 基序对于新出现的变体 BA.3.2 的裂解和肺细胞进入是可有可无的。

The conserved QTQTX motif in the SARS-CoV-2 spike protein is dispensable for cleavage and lung cell entry of the emerging variant BA.3.2.
作者： Lu Zhang, Nianzhen Chen, Amy Eichmann, Inga Nehlmeier, Ryan Hisner, Christine Happle, Georg M. N. Behrens, Markus Hoffmann, Stefan Pöhlmann
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 刺突 (S) 蛋白中的弗林蛋白酶基序对于进入肺细胞非常重要。它嵌入延伸的环结构中,前面有一个高度保守的 QTQTX 基序,这是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 WA-1 S 蛋白有效弗林蛋白酶裂解所必需的。BA.3.2是一种新兴的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2突变变体,于2026年4月在全球传播,编码高度突变的S蛋白。在这里,我们分析了 BA.3.2 的 刺突蛋白 裂解和肺细胞进入是否也需要 QTQTX 基序。我们报告了 BA.3.2 亚变体 BA.3.2.2 的两个源自患者的刺突序列缺乏 QTQTX 基序的第一个 QT 重复,并表明该基序对于 BA.3.2.2 的裂解和肺细胞进入在很大程度上是可有可无的,我们发现 BA.3.2.2 依赖于 TMPRSS2。我们的结果表明,持续感染期间 BA.3.2 S 蛋白的重新配置可能显着改变了弗林蛋白酶裂解的决定因素。重要性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 刺突 (S) 蛋白中的弗林蛋白酶基序在 sarbecovirus 中是独特的,并构成毒力决定因素。位于弗林蛋白酶基序上游的 QTQTX 基序是弗林蛋白酶裂解大流行早期传播的病毒 S 蛋白所必需的。在这里,我们表明 QTQTX 基序对于 S 蛋白加工和 S 蛋白驱动的新兴突变体 BA.3.2 进入肺细胞来说基本上是可有可无的,目前该突变体正在全球传播。因此,免疫受损个体内的 BA.3.2 进化 可能放宽了对 刺突蛋白 的弗林蛋白酶加工的要求。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在调查 QTQTX 基序(之前已证明是 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 WA-1 S 蛋白的弗林蛋白酶裂解所必需的)是否也是新兴 BA.3.2 变体的裂解和进入肺细胞所必需的。
设计与对象
该研究分析了 BA.3.2 亚变体 BA.3.2.2 的两个源自患者的刺突序列(缺少 QTQTX 基序的第一个 QT 重复序列),以评估它们对 刺突蛋白 裂解和肺细胞进入的影响。
核心方法
研究人员通过评估裂解和肺细胞进入来检查 QTQTX 基序在 BA.3.2 的 S 蛋白中的作用,发现这些过程依赖于 TMPRSS2 而不是 QTQTX 基序。
主要结果
结果表明,QTQTX 基序对于 BA.3.2.2 的裂解和肺细胞进入在很大程度上是可有可无的,这些过程依赖于 TMPRSS2。
解释与创新
作者将他们的发现解释为表明免疫功能低下个体中 BA.3.2 的 进化 可能改变了 刺突蛋白 的弗林蛋白酶加工要求。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能有助于了解 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体如何进化和适应,特别是在免疫功能低下的 宿主 中,并可能对 疫苗 和治疗开发产生影响。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于对两个患者来源的尖峰序列进行分析,并没有提供更广泛的普遍性,但考虑到 QTQTX 基序在 BA.3.2 中的作用的直接实验评估,证据是强有力的。

The conserved QTQTX motif in the SARS-CoV-2 spike protein is dispensable for cleavage and lung cell entry of the emerging variant BA.3.2.

Authors: Lu Zhang, Nianzhen Chen, Amy Eichmann, Inga Nehlmeier, Ryan Hisner, Christine Happle, Georg M. N. Behrens, Markus Hoffmann, Stefan Pöhlmann
Original abstract (English)
The furin motif in the SARS-CoV-2 spike (S) protein is important for lung cell entry. It is embedded in an extended loop structure and preceded by a highly conserved QTQTX motif that is required for efficient furin cleavage of the SARS-CoV-2 WA-1 S protein. BA.3.2 is an emerging SARS-CoV-2 saltation variant that is spreading globally in April 2026 and encodes a highly mutated S protein. Here, we analyzed whether the QTQTX motif is also required for spike protein cleavage and lung cell entry of BA.3.2. We report that two patient-derived spike sequences of the BA.3.2 subvariant BA.3.2.2 lack the first QT repeat of the QTQTX motif and show that this motif is largely dispensable for both cleavage and lung cell entry of BA.3.2.2, which we found to depend on TMPRSS2. Our results suggest that the reconfiguration of the BA.3.2 S protein during persistent infection may have significantly altered the determinants of furin cleavage.IMPORTANCEThe furin motif in the SARS-CoV-2 spike (S) protein is unique among sarbecoviruses and constitutes a virulence determinant. A QTQTX motif located immediately upstream of the furin motif is required for furin cleavage of the S protein of the virus that circulated early in the pandemic. Here, we show that the QTQTX motif is largely dispensable for S protein processing and S protein-driven lung cell entry of the emerging saltation variant BA.3.2, which is currently spreading globally. Thus, BA.3.2 evolution within immunocompromised individuals may have relaxed the requirements for furin processing of the spike protein.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate whether the QTQTX motif, previously shown to be required for furin cleavage of the SARS-CoV-2 WA-1 S protein, is also necessary for cleavage and lung cell entry of the emerging BA.3.2 variant.
Design and population
The study analyzed two patient-derived spike sequences of the BA.3.2 subvariant BA.3.2.2, which lack the first QT repeat of the QTQTX motif, to assess their impact on spike protein cleavage and lung cell entry.
Core methods
The researchers examined the role of the QTQTX motif in the S protein of BA.3.2 by assessing cleavage and lung cell entry, finding that these processes depend on TMPRSS2 rather than the QTQTX motif.
Main results
The results showed that the QTQTX motif is largely dispensable for both cleavage and lung cell entry of BA.3.2.2, with these processes being dependent on TMPRSS2.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted their findings as suggesting that the evolution of BA.3.2 within immunocompromised individuals may have altered the requirements for furin processing of the spike protein.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings may inform the understanding of how SARS-CoV-2 variants evolve and adapt, particularly in immunocompromised hosts, and could have implications for vaccine and therapeutic development.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to analysis of two patient-derived spike sequences and does not provide broader generalizability, but the evidence is strong given the direct experimental assessment of the QTQTX motif's role in BA.3.2.

使用基于表位的迁移学习对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 和 SARS-CoV-1 上的抗体识别进行建模

Modeling antibody recognition across SARS-CoV-2 and SARS-CoV-1 using epitope-informed transfer learning
作者： Jimmy B. Yuan, Ryan C. Bruneau, Stephen Won, Aidan Elliot Heller, Thomas Sheffield, Kenneth L. Sale, Brooke Harmon (247617), Le Thanh Mai Pham
摘要中文翻译抗病毒药物 抗体的发现受到抗体互补位和病毒表位巨大组合空间的限制,而搜索这些巨大可能性的新方法(例如用于抗体亲和力预测的机器学习模型)通常需要大量的特定目标数据集,而这些数据集很少可用于新兴病原体。为了解决这个问题,我们研究了迁移学习的能力,以减少所需的训练数据,同时仍然创建稳健的抗体亲和力预测模型。我们证明,通过结合迁移学习、以表位为中心的抗原编码、抗体 CDR 描述符和靶向实验验证,抗体亲和力预测可以从数据丰富的病毒靶点局部转移到相关数据有限的病毒靶点。使用 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 受体结合域 (RBD) 相互作用数据作为源域,我们开发了双编码器神经网络模型,该模型捕获抗体识别的功能决定因素,并支持预测 SARS-CoV-1 变体:抗体亲和力,尽管直接训练数据有限。高通量 ELISA 测量生成了涵盖纯重链抗体 (HCAb) 和传统抗体的紧凑验证数据集,从而通过相似性引导、模型知情的数据扩展实现迭代模型细化。在扩大的保留抗体评估中,最终模型显示出改进的但依赖于抗体的预测性能,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的平均皮尔逊相关性为 0.71 ± 0.12,SARS-CoV-1 的平均皮尔逊相关性为 0.63 ± 0.09,表现最好的坚持者分别达到 0.85 和 0.78。这些结果表明,现有的免疫学数据集可以支持特征受限的迁移学习,以对紧密相关的 Sarbecoviruses 之间的抗体:抗原相互作用进行数据有效的优先级排序,特别是当保守表位区域可以对齐并且有限的目标特异性测量可用于校准时。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在解决 抗病毒药物 抗体发现的挑战,该挑战受到抗体互补位和病毒表位的巨大组合空间以及新兴病原体的大型目标特异性数据集的缺乏的限制。
设计与对象
该研究调查了迁移学习的应用,以减少对大量训练数据的需求,同时创建稳健的抗体亲和力预测模型,重点关注 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 及其相关病毒 SARS-CoV-1。
核心方法
研究人员采用迁移学习、以表位为中心的抗原编码、抗体 CDR 描述符和有针对性的实验验证,使用高通量 ELISA 测量来生成涵盖纯重链抗体和常规抗体的紧凑验证数据集。
主要结果
最终模型证明了预测性能的提高,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的平均 Pearson 相关性为 0.71 ± 0.12,SARS-CoV-1 的平均 Pearson 相关性为 0.63 ± 0.09,表现最好的坚持者分别达到 0.85 和 0.78。
解释与创新
结果表明,现有的免疫学数据集可以支持特征受限的迁移学习,以对紧密相关的 Sarbecoviruses 之间的抗体:抗原相互作用进行数据有效的优先级排序,特别是当可以对齐保守表位区域并且可以校准有限的目标特异性测量时。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,迁移学习可以增强对相关病毒之间抗体识别的预测,这可能为跨变体治疗策略的开发提供信息,并改善新兴病原体的 监测。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对单一来源域(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RBD 相互作用数据)的依赖以及预测性能的抗体依赖性性质而受到限制,但考虑到使用高通量 ELISA 测量和有针对性的实验验证,证据是强有力的。

Modeling antibody recognition across SARS-CoV-2 and SARS-CoV-1 using epitope-informed transfer learning

Authors: Jimmy B. Yuan, Ryan C. Bruneau, Stephen Won, Aidan Elliot Heller, Thomas Sheffield, Kenneth L. Sale, Brooke Harmon (247617), Le Thanh Mai Pham
Original abstract (English)
Antiviral antibody discovery is constrained by the enormous combinatorial space of antibody paratopes and viral epitopes, while novel approaches to search for these vast possibilities such as machine learning models for antibody affinity prediction typically require large target-specific datasets that are rarely available for emerging pathogens. To address this problem, we investigated the ability of transfer learning to reduce the required training data and still create a robust predictive model of antibody affinities. We show that antibody-affinity prediction can be locally transferred from a data-rich viral target to a related data-limited viral target by combining transfer learning, epitope-centered antigen encoding, antibody CDR descriptors, and targeted experimental validation. Using SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding-domain (RBD) interaction data as the source domain, we developed dual-encoder neural-network models that capture functional determinants of antibody recognition and supports prediction of SARS-CoV-1 variant:antibody affinities despite limited direct training data. High-throughput ELISA measurements generated compact validation datasets spanning heavy-chain-only antibodies (HCAbs) and conventional antibodies, enabling iterative model refinement through similarity-guided, model-informed data expansion. Across expanded withheld-antibody evaluations, the final models showed improved but antibody-dependent predictive performance, with mean Pearson correlations of 0.71 ± 0.12 for SARS-CoV-2 and 0.63 ± 0.09 for SARS-CoV-1, and best-performing holdouts reaching 0.85 and 0.78, respectively. These results demonstrate that existing immunological datasets can support feature-constrained transfer learning for data-efficient prioritization of antibody:antigen interactions across closely related Sarbecoviruses, particularly when conserved epitope regions can be aligned and limited target-specific measurements are available for calibration.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the challenge of antiviral antibody discovery, which is limited by the vast combinatorial space of antibody paratopes and viral epitopes, and the scarcity of large target-specific datasets for emerging pathogens.
Design and population
The study investigated the application of transfer learning to reduce the need for extensive training data while creating a robust predictive model of antibody affinities, focusing on SARS-CoV-2 and its related virus SARS-CoV-1.
Core methods
The researchers employed transfer learning, epitope-centered antigen encoding, antibody CDR descriptors, and targeted experimental validation, using high-throughput ELISA measurements to generate compact validation datasets spanning heavy-chain-only antibodies and conventional antibodies.
Main results
The final models demonstrated improved predictive performance, with mean Pearson correlations of 0.71 ± 0.12 for SARS-CoV-2 and 0.63 ± 0.09 for SARS-CoV-1, with best-performing holdouts reaching 0.85 and 0.78, respectively.
Interpretation and novelty
The results show that existing immunological datasets can support feature-constrained transfer learning for data-efficient prioritization of antibody:antigen interactions across closely related Sarbecoviruses, particularly when conserved epitope regions can be aligned and limited target-specific measurements are available for calibration.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings suggest that transfer learning can enhance the prediction of antibody recognition across related viruses, which may inform the development of cross-variant therapeutic strategies and improve surveillance for emerging pathogens.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on a single source domain (SARS-CoV-2 RBD interaction data) and the antibody-dependent nature of predictive performance, but the evidence is strong given the use of high-throughput ELISA measurements and targeted experimental validation.

Outcomes of Liver Cirrhosis Patients Hospitalized for Influenza Compared With COVID-19 Between 2020 and 2021 in the United States.

Outcomes of Liver Cirrhosis Patients Hospitalized for Influenza Compared With COVID-19 Between 2020 and 2021 in the United States.
作者： Renuka Verma, Kamleshun Ramphul, Kyaw M Tun, Yasmeen Obeidat, Lily Liu, Hemamalini Sakthivel, Tejas Joshi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Patients with liver cirrhosis have been reported to experience severe outcomes from both COVID-19 and influenza, but comparative risks between these infections in this population remain insufficiently characterized in the United States.
设计与对象
This retrospective comparative study used the National Inpatient Sample (NIS) from 2016 to 2021 to identify cirrhosis patients hospitalized for influenza (2016–2019) or COVID-19 (2020–2021) in the United States.
核心方法
Baseline characteristics and outcomes were compared between propensity-score matched cohorts of cirrhosis patients with COVID-19 and influenza using the NIS dataset.
主要结果
Among 59,590 cirrhosis patients, 6,800 (11.4%) had influenza and 52,790 (88.6%) had COVID-19; after matching, 6,670 cases were included in each group, with COVID-19 patients showing higher risks of hyponatremia, acute myocardial infarction, cardiogenic shock, severe septic shock, mechanical ventilation, cardiac arrest, acute ischemic stroke, acute kidney injury, and all-cause death, but fewer cases of ascites and hepatorenal syndrome.
解释与创新
The study demonstrates that cirrhosis patients hospitalized with COVID-19 exhibit distinct complication profiles and higher mortality risk compared to those hospitalized with influenza, highlighting a need for targeted management strategies.
科研与公卫意义
These findings suggest that cirrhosis patients may require enhanced monitoring and preventive measures during COVID-19 surges, and that comparative risk assessment between respiratory viruses in this vulnerable group is essential for clinical decision-making.
局限与证据强度
对行政索赔数据的依赖可能会引入编码错误和无法测量的混杂,并且回顾性设计限制了因果推断;因此,证据强度中等。

Outcomes of Liver Cirrhosis Patients Hospitalized for Influenza Compared With COVID-19 Between 2020 and 2021 in the United States.

Authors: Renuka Verma, Kamleshun Ramphul, Kyaw M Tun, Yasmeen Obeidat, Lily Liu, Hemamalini Sakthivel, Tejas Joshi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Early studies during the COVID-19 pandemic showed that liver cirrhosis patients were at risk of more severe outcomes of the disease. Past studies have also linked cirrhosis patients with poorer influenza outcomes following admissions. AIM: To compare how patients with liver cirrhosis fared when admitted for COVID-19 as compared with influenza in the United States. METHODS: The National Inpatient Sample (NIS) from 2016 to 2021 was used. The influenza group of cirrhosis patients was extracted for admissions between 2016 and 2019, while the COVID-19 group involved hospitalizations in 2020 and 2021. Baseline characteristics and differences in outcomes were compared between cirrhosis patients with COVID-19 versus influenza. RESULTS: We recruited 59,590 cirrhosis patients, among whom 6800 (11.4%) had influenza and 52790 (88.6%) were infected with COVID-19. COVID-19 cases were younger, with more patients having dyslipidemia, diabetes, chronic kidney disease (CKD), obesity, and dementia, but fewer patients were smokers, peripheral vascular disease (PVD), history of stroke, history of myocardial infarction, drug abuse, alcohol abuse, chronic ischemic heart disease (IHD), asthma, COPD, and cancer. Our propensity-score matched sample had 6670 cases with COVID-19 and 6670 with influenza. COVID-19 patients were more likely to suffer from hyponatremia, acute myocardial infarction, cardiogenic shock, severe septic shock, mechanical ventilation use, prolonged mechanical ventilation use, cardiac arrest, acute ischemic stroke, acute kidney injury, and all-cause death, but fewer cases with ascites, and hepatorenal syndrome. CONCLUSIONS: The characteristics of cirrhosis patients admitted with COVID-19 differed from those admitted for influenza, with COVID-19 showing a higher mortality risk and varying differences in other complications.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Patients with liver cirrhosis have been reported to experience severe outcomes from both COVID-19 and influenza, but comparative risks between these infections in this population remain insufficiently characterized in the United States.
Design and population
This retrospective comparative study used the National Inpatient Sample (NIS) from 2016 to 2021 to identify cirrhosis patients hospitalized for influenza (2016–2019) or COVID-19 (2020–2021) in the United States.
Core methods
Baseline characteristics and outcomes were compared between propensity-score matched cohorts of cirrhosis patients with COVID-19 and influenza using the NIS dataset.
Main results
Among 59,590 cirrhosis patients, 6,800 (11.4%) had influenza and 52,790 (88.6%) had COVID-19; after matching, 6,670 cases were included in each group, with COVID-19 patients showing higher risks of hyponatremia, acute myocardial infarction, cardiogenic shock, severe septic shock, mechanical ventilation, cardiac arrest, acute ischemic stroke, acute kidney injury, and all-cause death, but fewer cases of ascites and hepatorenal syndrome.
Interpretation and novelty
The study demonstrates that cirrhosis patients hospitalized with COVID-19 exhibit distinct complication profiles and higher mortality risk compared to those hospitalized with influenza, highlighting a need for targeted management strategies.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings suggest that cirrhosis patients may require enhanced monitoring and preventive measures during COVID-19 surges, and that comparative risk assessment between respiratory viruses in this vulnerable group is essential for clinical decision-making.
Limitations and evidence strength
The reliance on administrative claims data may introduce coding errors and unmeasured confounding, and the retrospective design limits causal inference; the evidence strength is therefore moderate.

Risk Factors of the Spread of COVID-19 in Prisoners: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

Risk Factors of the Spread of COVID-19 in Prisoners: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.
作者： Seyedahmad Seyedalinaghi, Esmaeil Mehraeen, Farid Farahani Rad, Haleh Siami, Mohammad Javaherian, Muhammad Ali Rasheed, Amirbehzad Bagheri, Ali Zand, Elham Emamgholizadeh Baboli, Kavian Rostami
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to identify and synthesize evidence on risk factors for COVID-19 in incarcerated populations.
证据基础与方法
截至 2025 年 10 月,我们对 PubMed、Scopus 和 Google Scholar 中的英文文章进行了系统检索。使用 PRISMA 检查表和 NOS 工具评估研究质量。使用随机效应模型在 Stata 17 中进行荟萃分析,以优势比作为主要效应指标,统计显着性设置为 P < 0.05。
共识与结论
The total sample size was 176169, consisting of 169117 males and 7,726 females. In most studies, the average age was reported to be around 40 years. Fully vaccinated prisoners were less likely to contract COVID-19 than unvaccinated ones (OR: 0.38; 95% CI: 0.22, 0.67; P = 0.001), while no significant relationships were found regarding sex and age. Our findings confirm that full vaccination significantly protects against COVID-19 in prisons.
争议与缺口
通过 I2 统计评估异质性。
科研与实践启示
优先开展 疫苗接种 活动以及使用口罩和手部卫生等措施对于保护被监禁者和更广泛的社区至关重要。

Risk Factors of the Spread of COVID-19 in Prisoners: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis.

Authors: Seyedahmad Seyedalinaghi, Esmaeil Mehraeen, Farid Farahani Rad, Haleh Siami, Mohammad Javaherian, Muhammad Ali Rasheed, Amirbehzad Bagheri, Ali Zand, Elham Emamgholizadeh Baboli, Kavian Rostami
Original abstract (English)
OBJECTIVES: This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to identify and synthesize evidence on risk factors for COVID-19 in incarcerated populations. METHODS: A systematic search of PubMed, Scopus, and Google Scholar was conducted for English articles until October 2025. Study quality was assessed using the PRISMA checklist and NOS tool. Meta-analysis was performed in Stata 17 using a random-effects model, with odds ratios as the primary effect measure and statistical significance set at P < 0.05. Heterogeneity was evaluated via I2 statistics. RESULTS: Twelve articles were ultimately included in the study. For the meta-analysis, only 5 articles were considered, which evaluated sex, age, and vaccination status as the risk factors of COVID-19. The total sample size was 176169, consisting of 169117 males and 7,726 females. In most studies, the average age was reported to be around 40 years. Fully vaccinated prisoners were less likely to contract COVID-19 than unvaccinated ones (OR: 0.38; 95% CI: 0.22, 0.67; P = 0.001), while no significant relationships were found regarding sex and age. CONCLUSIONS: Our findings confirm that full vaccination significantly protects against COVID-19 in prisons. Prioritizing vaccination campaigns, along with measures like mask use and hand hygiene, is essential to protect incarcerated individuals and the wider community.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to identify and synthesize evidence on risk factors for COVID-19 in incarcerated populations.
Evidence base and method
A systematic search of PubMed, Scopus, and Google Scholar was conducted for English articles until October 2025. Study quality was assessed using the PRISMA checklist and NOS tool. Meta-analysis was performed in Stata 17 using a random-effects model, with odds ratios as the primary effect measure and statistical significance set at P < 0.05.
Consensus and conclusions
The total sample size was 176169, consisting of 169117 males and 7,726 females. In most studies, the average age was reported to be around 40 years. Fully vaccinated prisoners were less likely to contract COVID-19 than unvaccinated ones (OR: 0.38; 95% CI: 0.22, 0.67; P = 0.001), while no significant relationships were found regarding sex and age. Our findings confirm that full vaccination significantly protects against COVID-19 in prisons.
Controversies and gaps
Heterogeneity was evaluated via I2 statistics.
Research and practice implications
Prioritizing vaccination campaigns, along with measures like mask use and hand hygiene, is essential to protect incarcerated individuals and the wider community.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.
作者： Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
证据基础与方法
进行了系统评价和个体参与者数据荟萃分析,检索了 2020 年 1 月至 2026 年 1 月的电子数据库。该方案在 PROSPERO 注册(CRD420251142891)。纳入了 47 例病例(12 项已发表研究中的 30 例和 17 例未发表病例),资格标准包括免疫功能低下患者在 mAb 治疗后 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 持续呈阳性。
共识与结论
70.2% (33/47) 的 mAb 耐药性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染患者在 CCP 输注后发生 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 清除,平均输注 2.7 个 CCP 单位。在逻辑回归分析中,CCP 输注总量与病毒清除率呈正相关。
争议与缺口
该综述没有报告所纳入病例中涉及的特定 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体,限制了评估 CCP 变体特异性疗效的能力。此外,样本量小(n = 47)和包含未发表的病例可能会引入选择偏差,并且缺乏对照组会妨碍明确的因果推断。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先考虑随机对照试验,以验证 CCP 对单克隆抗体失败后持续 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的免疫功能低下患者的疗效,并按变异类型和 CCP 滴度进行分层。监测 计划应监测 CCP 针对新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的功效,以指导临床使用。

Convalescent Plasma Restores Viral Clearance After Anti-Spike Monoclonal Antibody Failure in Immunocompromised COVID-19 Patients: A Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis of Individual Patient Data.

Authors: Daniele Focosi, Tommaso Francesco Aiello, Arturo Casadevall, Mario Cruciani, Alessandra D'Abramo, Patrizia De Marco, David Gachoud, Carolina Garcia-Vidal, Claudia Glingani, Thomas Hueso
Original abstract (English)
Anti-Spike monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) progressively lost efficacy during the COVID-19 pandemic due to the emergence and predominance of resistant SARS-CoV-2 variants. By contrast, high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) collected from vaccinated donors recently recovered from infection provides a polyclonal source of antibodies that remains effective in clearing SARS-CoV-2 in immunosuppressed patients, unable to mount an adequate immune response against the virus. We conducted a systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis to examine the effect of CCP in immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following mAb therapy, and to evaluate possible biological factors associated with a favourable outcome. Electronic databases were searched for studies published from January 2020 to January 2026. All studies including eligible cases were considered in the systematic review. Unpublished cases were also collected from investigators of the selected studies. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 cases from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included. In 33 out of 47 patients (70.2%) with a mAb-resistant infection, SARS-CoV-2 clearance occurred following CCP transfusion, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. In logistic regression, total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance. In conclusion, CCP transfusion was associated with SARS-CoV-2 clearance in persistently positive immunocompromised patients following failure of anti-Spike mAb therapy.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the efficacy of high-titre COVID-19 convalescent plasma (CCP) in restoring viral clearance among immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 viraemia following failure of anti-Spike monoclonal antibody (mAb) therapy, focusing on the central question of whether CCP can overcome resistance to mAbs in this population.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and individual participant data meta-analysis was conducted, searching electronic databases from January 2020 to January 2026. The protocol was registered at PROSPERO (CRD420251142891). Forty-seven cases (30 from 12 published studies and 17 unpublished cases) were included, with eligibility criteria encompassing immunocompromised patients persistently positive for SARS-CoV-2 after mAb therapy.
Consensus and conclusions
In 70.2% of patients (33/47) with mAb-resistant SARS-CoV-2 infection, SARS-CoV-2 clearance occurred following CCP transfusion, with a mean of 2.7 CCP units administered. Total CCP volume transfused was positively associated with viral clearance in logistic regression analysis.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not report the specific SARS-CoV-2 variants involved in the included cases, limiting the ability to assess variant-specific efficacy of CCP. Additionally, the small sample size (n=47) and inclusion of unpublished cases may introduce selection bias, and the lack of a control group precludes definitive causal inference.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize randomized controlled trials to validate the efficacy of CCP in immunocompromised patients with persistent SARS-CoV-2 infection post-mAb failure, stratifying by variant type and CCP titre. Surveillance programs should monitor CCP efficacy against emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants to guide clinical use.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.
作者： Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19 has been contested, with large randomized trials producing conflicting results that led to recommendations against routine use.
证据基础与方法
我们通过设计分层叙述综合(PRISMA 2020;SWiM;OSF:https://osf.io/tjcuq)进行了系统评价,以测试是否可以通过三个相互作用的决定因素(时间、抗体滴度和 宿主 特征)来调和这些相互冲突的结果,并使用应用于每项研究的标准化 91 场提取矩阵。
共识与结论
合成结果表明,CP 是有效的,但仅在特定条件下有效:在病程早期,以足够的抗体滴度,对尚未产生内源性抗体反应的血清阴性或免疫功能低下的 宿主 进行给药。
争议与缺口
第二个先前未量化的发现涉及抗体类别:几乎不存在非 IgG 免疫球蛋白的表征,尽管有证据表明 IgA 和 IgM 是针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的最有效中和类别之一,但我们小组的 31 项研究中只有 2 项研究报告了 IgA 或 IgM 数据。这一假设生成观察发现了 CP 产品评估中的系统性差距。
科研与实践启示
这些发现将CP疗效从二元问题重新定义为条件问题,并为未来大流行中精确部署被动免疫疗法提供了模板。

Timing, Titre, and Host Determinants of Convalescent Plasma Efficacy in COVID-19: A Systematic Review With Design-Stratified Narrative Synthesis.

Authors: Agnieszka Kuś, Sebastian Makuch, Jarosław Dybko, Siddarth Agrawal
Original abstract (English)
The efficacy of convalescent plasma (CP) in COVID-19 has been contested, with large randomized trials producing conflicting results that led to recommendations against routine use. We performed a systematic review with design-stratified narrative synthesis (PRISMA 2020; SWiM; OSF: https://osf.io/tjcuq) to test whether these conflicting outcomes can be reconciled through three interacting determinants-timing, antibody titre, and host characteristics-using a standardized 91-field extraction matrix applied to each study. PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus were searched through May 2026. Thirty-one studies (20,793 patients; 8 RCTs, 23 observational) were included. The synthesis indicates that CP is effective, but only under a specific convergence of conditions: when administered early in the disease course, with sufficient antibody titre, to seronegative or immunocompromised hosts who have not yet mounted an endogenous antibody response. Among RCTs analysing timing, 4 of 6 found benefit with earlier administration; for titre, 3 of 4 RCTs with explicit comparisons found benefit with higher-titre CP; and two large RCTs (combined n = 12,522) demonstrated preferential benefit in seronegative recipients. This interpretation is consistent with modelling estimates that CP, deployed largely to hospitalised patients, nonetheless saved tens of thousands of lives in the United States during the first pandemic year, an effect attributed to the subset treated early enough to modify disease progression. A second, previously unquantified finding concerns antibody class: characterisation of non-IgG immunoglobulins was nearly absent, with only 2 of 31 studies-both from our group-reporting IgA or IgM data, despite evidence that IgA and IgM are among the most potent neutralising classes against SARS-CoV-2. This hypothesis-generating observation identifies a systematic gap in CP product assessment. These findings reframe CP efficacy from a binary question to a conditional one and provide a template for the precision deployment of passive immunotherapy in future pandemics.
View review five-element analysis

Inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of critically ill COVID-19 patients is not associated with increased mortality: a systematic review with exploratory meta-analysis

Inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of critically ill COVID-19 patients is not associated with increased mortality: a systematic review with exploratory meta-analysis
作者： Jonas Brock, Harald Lux, Michael Bauer, Petra Dickmann
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This systematic review aims to determine whether inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of adult COVID-19 patients is associated with increased mortality or other adverse clinical outcomes.
证据基础与方法
We systematically searched PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus for observational studies comparing clinical outcomes of adult COVID-19 patients who underwent inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer with non-transferred ICU patients. Two reviewers screened, selected, and extracted data independently and in duplicate. Risk of bias was assessed using the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale and ROBINS-I, and certainty of evidence using GRADE. Exploratory random-effects meta-analyses were performed separately for each effect measure, with Hartung-Knapp and crude-effect sensitivity analyses.
共识与结论
The available observational evidence did not show a clear increase in mortality among transferred compared with non-transferred COVID-19 ICU patients. None of the adjusted effect estimates showed a statistically significant mortality disadvantage for transferred patients. Pooled adjusted subgroup estimates were an odds ratio of 1.29 (95% CI 0.84 to 1.98; k = 3) and a hazard ratio of 0.71 (95% CI 0.34 to 1.46; k = 2).
争议与缺口
在大多数研究中,转院患者的住院时间更长。提供的证据没有报告这些研究是否解释了患者严重程度或医院资源的差异。提供的证据没有报告荟萃分析是否考虑了研究设计或人群特征的异质性。
科研与实践启示
这些发现支持使用医院间转运作为管理 ICU 激增容量的安全策略。所提供的证据未报告研究、监测、诊断、治疗、疫苗接种、生态、风险评估或政策的具体优先事项。

Inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of critically ill COVID-19 patients is not associated with increased mortality: a systematic review with exploratory meta-analysis

Authors: Jonas Brock, Harald Lux, Michael Bauer, Petra Dickmann
Original abstract (English)
During the COVID-19 pandemic, inter-hospital transfer of critically ill patients between intensive care units was widely used to manage capacity shortages. While several individual studies have compared outcomes of transferred and non-transferred patients, no systematic synthesis of this evidence exists. This systematic review aims to determine whether inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of adult COVID-19 patients is associated with increased mortality or other adverse clinical outcomes. We systematically searched PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus for observational studies comparing clinical outcomes of adult COVID-19 patients who underwent inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer with non-transferred ICU patients. Two reviewers screened, selected, and extracted data independently and in duplicate. Risk of bias was assessed using the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale and ROBINS-I, and certainty of evidence using GRADE. Exploratory random-effects meta-analyses were performed separately for each effect measure, with Hartung-Knapp and crude-effect sensitivity analyses. Nine observational studies from seven countries were included (approximately 6,100 transferred and 29,200 non-transferred ICU patients). None of the adjusted effect estimates showed a statistically significant mortality disadvantage for transferred patients. Pooled adjusted subgroup estimates were an odds ratio of 1.29 (95% CI 0.84 to 1.98; k = 3) and a hazard ratio of 0.71 (95% CI 0.34 to 1.46; k = 2). Length of stay was longer in transferred patients in most studies. The available observational evidence did not show a clear increase in mortality among transferred compared with non-transferred COVID-19 ICU patients. These findings support the use of inter-hospital transfer as a safe strategy for managing ICU surge capacity. PROSPERO CRD420261356915.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review aims to determine whether inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer of adult COVID-19 patients is associated with increased mortality or other adverse clinical outcomes.
Evidence base and method
We systematically searched PubMed, Web of Science, and Scopus for observational studies comparing clinical outcomes of adult COVID-19 patients who underwent inter-hospital ICU-to-ICU transfer with non-transferred ICU patients. Two reviewers screened, selected, and extracted data independently and in duplicate. Risk of bias was assessed using the Newcastle-Ottawa Scale and ROBINS-I, and certainty of evidence using GRADE. Exploratory random-effects meta-analyses were performed separately for each effect measure, with Hartung-Knapp and crude-effect sensitivity analyses.
Consensus and conclusions
The available observational evidence did not show a clear increase in mortality among transferred compared with non-transferred COVID-19 ICU patients. None of the adjusted effect estimates showed a statistically significant mortality disadvantage for transferred patients. Pooled adjusted subgroup estimates were an odds ratio of 1.29 (95% CI 0.84 to 1.98; k = 3) and a hazard ratio of 0.71 (95% CI 0.34 to 1.46; k = 2).
Controversies and gaps
Length of stay was longer in transferred patients in most studies. The supplied evidence does not report whether the studies accounted for differences in patient severity or hospital resources. The supplied evidence does not report whether the meta-analyses considered heterogeneity in study design or population characteristics.
Research and practice implications
These findings support the use of inter-hospital transfer as a safe strategy for managing ICU surge capacity. The supplied evidence does not report specific priorities for research, surveillance, diagnosis, treatment, vaccination, ecology, risk assessment or policy.

Reviewer #2 (Public review): Nanoscopy reveals heparan sulfate clusters as docking sites for SARS-CoV-2 attachment and entry
作者： Sue Han, Xin Wang, Tiansheng Li, Ammar Mohseni, Ivan Kosik, Chuang Yu Chan, Alberto Domingo López-Muñoz, Jessica Matthias, Reid Suddaby, Zhixiong Wang
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
For SARS-CoV-2 underlying the COVID-19 pandemic, angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) is widely considered the receptor for cell-surface attachment and cytosolic entry.
证据基础与方法
提供的证据没有明确说明审查方法、数据库、资格流程或证据基础。
共识与结论
使用先进的光学显微镜来解析单个病毒体和受体,我们发现硫酸乙酰肝素 (HS),而不是 ACE2,介导 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 细胞表面附着和随后的内吞作用。

我们发现了病毒进入的两种受体:硫酸乙酰肝素,一种可以结合许多病毒的多糖,介导病毒附着和随后的内吞作用,而血管紧张素转换酶2(ACE2),通常认为的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体,仅在下游起作用以促进病毒感染。

Reviewer #2 (Public review): Nanoscopy reveals heparan sulfate clusters as docking sites for SARS-CoV-2 attachment and entry

Authors: Sue Han, Xin Wang, Tiansheng Li, Ammar Mohseni, Ivan Kosik, Chuang Yu Chan, Alberto Domingo López-Muñoz, Jessica Matthias, Reid Suddaby, Zhixiong Wang
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Virus entry is thought to involve binding a unique receptor for cell attachment and cytosolic entry. For SARS-CoV-2 underlying the COVID-19 pandemic, angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) is widely considered the receptor for cell-surface attachment and cytosolic entry. Using advanced light microscopy to resolve individual virions and receptors, we found instead that heparan sulfate (HS), not ACE2, mediates SARS-CoV-2 cell-surface attachment and subsequent endocytosis. ACE2 functions only downstream of HS to enable viral genome expression. Instead of binding single HS molecules that electrostatically interact with viral surface proteins weakly, SARS-CoV-2 binds clusters of ∼6–137 HS molecules projecting 60–410 nm above the plasma membrane. These tall, HS-rich clusters, present at about one per 6 μm2, act as docking sites for viral attachment. Blocking HS binding with the clinically used HS-binding agent pixantrone strongly inhibited an authentic pathogen, the SARS-CoV-2 Omicron JN.1 subvariant, from attaching to and infecting human airway cells. This work establishes a revised entry paradigm in which HS clusters mediate SARS-CoV-2 attachment and endocytosis, with ACE2 acting downstream, thereby identifying HS interactions as a key anti-COVID-19 strategy. This paradigm and its therapeutic implications may apply broadly beyond COVID-19 because, analogous to SARS-CoV-2, HS binds many other viruses but is only considered an attachment regulator. Statement of Significance Viral entry, a crucial antiviral target, is typically thought to involve binding its unique receptor for the cell surface attachment and subsequent entry. We examined this concept with advanced microscopies to resolve individual receptors and SARS-CoV-2 virions responsible for the COVID-19 pandemic. We discovered two receptors for viral entry: heparan sulfate, a polysaccharide that may bind many viruses, mediates viral attachment and subsequent endocytosis, whereas angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2), the generally assumed SARS-CoV-2 receptor, acts only downstream to facilitate viral infection. This model suggests perturbation of HS binding as a more effective anti-COVID-19 strategy than previously recognized. It may apply broadly beyond COVID-19 because, analogous to SARS-CoV-2, HS binds many other viruses but is only considered an attachment regulator.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
For SARS-CoV-2 underlying the COVID-19 pandemic, angiotensin-converting enzyme 2 (ACE2) is widely considered the receptor for cell-surface attachment and cytosolic entry.
Evidence base and method
The supplied evidence does not clearly state the review method, databases, eligibility process, or evidence base.
Consensus and conclusions

Controversies and gaps

Research and practice implications
This model suggests perturbation of HS binding as a more effective anti-COVID-19 strategy than previously recognized.
